Driven by `[cloud-frontdoor]` per nubs's go, coordinated on #8434. — `[cloud-frontdoor]`17:T12a8,Closes #11670

## Root cause

`useChatSend`'s post-turn reconcile (`loadConversationMessages`) **full-replaces** the thread with server truth. When the server never persisted the user's turn — a send during local-model warm-up where the runtime-ready hold expired (`503 Agent is not running` from `conversation-routes.ts`; the user message is only persisted *after* runtime resolution), or a stream that completed empty with nothing persisted — the reload returned a thread **without** the user's message and silently evicted the optimistic bubble. No failed state, no notice on the empty-success variant, text unrecoverable. The suite never caught it because its `loadConversationMessages` mock was a no-op `{ ok: true }` instead of the real full-replace.

## Fix (root cause, no fallback sludge)

- **`restoreEvictedUserTurn()`** — after every post-send reconcile, detect that the just-sent user turn is missing from server truth (text match among user turns no older than the send minus 60s clock-skew slack, so an identical message from an *earlier* exchange can't mask an eviction) and re-attach the user's bubble **plus a retryable failed assistant turn** (`failureKind: "provider_issue"` → the overlay's existing Retry chip). The send fails loudly; one tap re-delivers once the model is ready — mirroring the existing `reattachInterruptedPartial` pattern for assistant partials.
- Wired into both reload sites of `runQueuedChatSend` (success + non-404 error path) and both sites of `sendActionMessage`. Validation-4xx rejects excluded (their draft goes back to the composer — re-attaching would duplicate). No-op when the server persisted the turn or the reload never replaced local state.
- `handleChatRetry` fallback now also drops the optimistic (`temp-`) user turn it retries, so the resend's fresh optimistic bubble doesn't transiently duplicate the message.

19:T1324,Closes #11612.

On-device local LLM on iPhone 16 Pro Max (A18 Pro) crash-looped: `EXC_BAD_ACCESS (SIGSEGV) KERN_INVALID_ADDRESS at 0x0` in `ggml_metal_encoder_set_pipeline` during the first `llama_decode`, ~32 s after every launch, 4/4 launches (`.github/issue-evidence/xplatform-ios-device/prefix-App-2026-07-02-182502.ips` and siblings).

## The fix (three parts + root cause)

**1. Recoverable Metal failure** — llama.cpp fork submodule → `58c0391ebbe` (branch [`fix/11612-metal-nil-pipeline-recoverable`](https://github.com/elizaOS/llama.cpp/tree/fix/11612-metal-nil-pipeline-recoverable) on `elizaOS/llama.cpp`). A nil compute pipeline no longer aborts the process: the encoder latches the failure, the op skips encoding, and `ggml_metal_graph_compute` returns `GGML_STATUS_FAILED`, so `llama_decode` surfaces a catchable error (clean error / cloud fallback — no crash-loop). `GGML_METAL_ABORT_ON_NIL_PIPELINE=1` restores the old abort for debugging. This supersedes develop's previous gitlink `299d5b78b` (abort-only guard); the new tip is a clean superset that also carries the non-Apple kokoro fast path + converter work already on the fork branch.

**2. GGML log file sink** — iOS doesn't forward the embedded engine's stdio, so the `GGML_LOG_ERROR` naming the failing kernel was unobservable. `runtime-symbol-shim.c eliza_llama_log_to_file` + `LlamaBridgeImpl installGgmlLogSink` now append ggml/llama logs line-flushed to `$ELIZA_STATE_DIR/logs/ggml.log`, pullable with `xcrun devicectl device copy from`. This sink is what captured the root cause below.

**3. Jetsam memory budget** — `LlamaBridgeImpl.loadModel` measures `os_proc_available_memory()` before loading, halves context/KV down to 1024 to fit, and fails cleanly with a memory error when even the minimum cannot fit; the iOS JS bridge (`plugin-capacitor-bridge/src/ios/bridge.ts`) pre-flights model size vs available RAM before starting a multi-minute native load.

**bf16 root cause** (found via the new log sink): the iOS embedded metallib was compiled at `-std=ios-metal2.4`, and `ggml-metal.metal` `#if`'s the whole bf16 kernel family out below `__METAL_VERSION__ 310` — so `kernel_mul_mm_bf16_f32` was missing from the library while the A18 GPU-family probe still set `has_bfloat=true` and selected it for eliza-1's bf16 tensors → `MTLLibraryError Code=5`, every decode graph dead. Fix chain:
- `build-llama-cpp-mtp.mjs`: default `ELIZA_IOS_METAL_STD` `ios-metal2.4` → `metal3.1` (raises the AIR floor to iOS 17; on iOS 16 the embedded library fails to load and Metal cleanly degrades to CPU).
- submodule `58c0391ebbe`: gate `has_bfloat` on the loaded library **actually containing** the bf16 kernels, not just the GPU family.
- `build-xcframework.mjs`: packaging audit that hard-fails if `kernel_mul_mm_bf16_f32` is missing from a slice — this regression class can't ship silently again.

## On-device retest (same physical device, rebuilt + redeployed each leg)

- **Crash-loop: GONE, device-verified.** Post-fix: **zero new `App-*.ips`** across ~20 min / two launches including full model load + the first `llama_decode` (the exact pre-fix crash point) — the decode failure is logged and surfaced instead. Evidence: [`11612-fix-retest/`](../tree/fix/11612-ios-metal-recoverable-bf16/.github/issue-evidence/xplatform-ios-device/11612-fix-retest) (README, soak screenshots t+60→780 s, `ggml-device.log`, crash-baseline vs final crash-list diff).
- **bf16 kernel: RESOLVED on device.** After the MSL 3.1 rebuild, `kernel_mul_mm_bf16_f32` compiles and loads on the A18 (`ggml_metal_library_compile_pipeline: loaded kernel_mul_mm_bf16_f32_… th_max = 1024`), zero missing-kernel errors in the new session, zero new app crash reports. Evidence: [`11612-bf16-retest/`](../tree/fix/11612-ios-metal-recoverable-bf16/.github/issue-evidence/xplatform-ios-device/11612-bf16-retest) (README, `ggml-device-postfix.log`, `mtp-slice-capabilities.json` packaging audit PASS).
- **Known residual (separate product work, quantified in the bf16 README):** generation on the 8 GB phone is still blocked by GPU OOM — a 4.63 GB fp16-class GGUF at `n_gpu_layers: 999` + `n_ubatch` 1024 demands ~5.8 GiB against a 5.46 GiB Metal working-set budget. Needs device-aware offload/ubatch admission (iOS has no `assessFit` equivalent) and/or a phone-tier quant + unload-on-decode-failure. The app no longer crashes: the failure is a clean logged error.

1c:Tc07,## What this is

An end-to-end pipeline that fine-tunes **eliza-1-2b** on trajectories harvested from **gpt-5.5 (via the Codex subscription)** running our own scenario/benchmark/e2e harness — so the model specializes on our scenarios and tool-calling shape — plus every launcher/infra bug found and fixed along the way.

## Pipeline (all stages proven)

1. **gpt-5.5-via-Codex harness routing** — `ELIZA_CHAT_VIA_CLI=codex` → `plugin-cli-inference` → `codex exec -m gpt-5.5` serves the scenario runner live (proven with a real trajectory).
2. **Harvest** — 946 scenarios + benchmarks + e2e lanes run through gpt-5.5 → **208 passing trajectories** (`scripts/training-harvest/*`).
3. **GEPA** — Genetic-Pareto prompt optimization over the failing scenarios; solved the Cerebras rate-limit wall (paced adapter + row-slimming) and recovered scenarios.
4. **Dataset** — **1,667 `eliza_native_v1` rows** (train/val/test) pushed to `elizaos/eliza-1-training-data:converted/gpt55-scenarios/`.
5. **Fine-tune** — APOLLO SFT of eliza-1-2b on a scenario-heavy combined corpus (55% our gpt-5.5 scenarios + 45% base eliza-1) on a Nebius H200, box auto-torn-down.
6. **GGUF** — deployable **q4_k_m (3.2 GB)**, verified generating coherent text at **157 tok/s on Metal** (`packages/training/scripts/_gguf_local.sh`).
7. **Benchmark** — base vs fine-tuned native-tool-call/JSON structure adherence **50% → 62.5%** (directional; small local MPS sample — see `BENCH_RESULTS.md`).

## Launcher / infra bugs fixed (each committed separately)

- **`train_nebius.sh`**: strip `--info=progress2` (macOS openrsync rejects it → corpus sync + checkpoint fetch silently failed); fetch the remote run log (failures were undiagnosable); skip the 21 GB optimizer state in the fetch (kept the billing H200 alive ~1.3 h).
- **`train_local.py` + `native_tool_call_bench.py`**: base Gemma-4 tokenizers ship **no chat_template** → borrow it from the `-it` instruct variant (SFT + bench both render now); normalize tool_calls to the template's `function`-nested shape.
- **`plugin-training`**: pace the Cerebras eval adapter (concurrency gate + delay + 65 s 429-quota backoff) so GEPA fan-out fits under the queue/TPM ceilings; planner scorer reads `toolName`.
- **`plugin-cli-inference`**: add the `eliza-source` export condition so the cli/codex provider resolves in source-mode worktrees.
- **benchmarks `bench.ts` + app-core live-provider**: add a cli/codex branch so benchmarks + live-agent e2e can run on gpt-5.5-via-Codex.

## Evidence

Under `.github/issue-evidence/gpt55-training-pipeline/` — provider proof, harvest verdicts, GEPA results, the assembled dataset manifest, `s5-gguf/GGUF_RESULTS.md` (conversion + 157 tok/s generation), `s5-eval/BENCH_RESULTS.md` + raw `base.json`/`ft.json`.

1d:T15ae,Closes #11856.

## What this does

Ports the [Vexa](https://github.com/Vexa-ai/vexa) (Apache-2.0) meeting-bot stack **into the elizaOS plugin system** — no separate deployment. The agent joins **Google Meet / Microsoft Teams / Zoom** meetings as an anonymous guest, captures per-speaker audio in the browser, transcribes it through the runtime model layer (`ModelType.TRANSCRIPTION`), attributes speakers by DOM-based diarization, and lands live, diarized transcripts in the existing **Transcripts view** and **knowledge store**. **Discord** voice channels get the same treatment (per-user SSRC = exact diarization). Works fully **local** (no cloud, no OAuth), hybrid, and fully **cloud** (metered `/api/v1/voice/stt`).

## Architecture

New **`@elizaos/plugin-meetings`** — one engine, per-platform strategy classes (Vexa's proven `PlatformStrategies` shape):

- **platforms/** — ported bot adapters (Meet/Teams/Zoom): guest join, admission (reCAPTCHA-stay), removal, leave, verbatim Vexa selectors; `playwright-core` stealth launch; pluggable humanized input (Playwright min-jerk trajectories / Linux XTEST). Browser audio capture: per-element 16 kHz `ScriptProcessor` via `exposeBinding`.
- **pipeline/** — `SpeakerStreamManager` (LocalAgreement-2 confirmation, 2s cadence / 30s cap / 15s idle), ASR via `useModel(TRANSCRIPTION)` with retry/backoff, verbatim multi-language hallucination corpus, `TranscriptSegment` assembly + retained session mix.
- **service.ts** — session state machine (single bot per meeting, room/world/entity wiring), `JOIN_MEETING`/`LEAVE_MEETING`/`GET_MEETING_TRANSCRIPT` actions, `/api/meetings` routes, `ACTIVE_MEETINGS` provider, live `meeting-status`/`meeting-transcript` WS envelopes via the existing `connector-setup` broadcast seam (no `packages/agent` change).

Storage reuses the existing transcript subsystem (#8789): records land in the `transcripts` memory partition **and** mirror to the documents/knowledge store, so `/api/transcripts` + the Transcripts view render meeting transcripts with zero extra wiring (golden-tested against the view reader).

**Integrations:**
- **plugin-discord** — voice-channel meeting transcripts: tees each speaker's decoded PCM into the pipeline; `/transcribe start|stop`; `DISCORD_VOICE_TRANSCRIPTS` gate (default off); existing utterance-reply/barge-in/TTS untouched.
- **plugin-elizacloud** — registers the previously-dormant `handleTranscription` so `useModel(TRANSCRIPTION)` arbitrates local-first with Eliza Cloud fallback; `ELIZAOS_CLOUD_USE_STT` gate mirroring the TTS gate.
- **plugin-calendar / plugin-google** — meeting auto-join via the frozen `ScheduledTask` spine (`relative_to_anchor` on `calendar_event`, anchors follow reschedules; `off|ask|all` policy); Google `conferenceData` fix (prefer video entry point over dial-in); "Send agent to meeting" + in-meeting badge in the calendar views.
- **packages/ui** — Transcripts view gains a paste-URL Join bar, live confirmed+pending pane over the agent WS, platform/participant metadata on meeting records.

Local-only: anonymous guest join + local fused ASR, zero cloud/OAuth. Telegram group-call audio is **not** reachable via the Bot API (no tgcalls) — documented limitation, MTProto is future work.

Attribution: `NOTICE` file credits Vexa.ai (Apache-2.0).

## Testing

Per-package (all typecheck clean): **plugin-meetings 119**, **plugin-discord +26**, **plugin-calendar +25**, **plugin-elizacloud transcription 12**, **packages/ui transcripts 32** — real-logic unit/e2e (LocalAgreement-2, vote-and-lock, caption routing, Pulse chunking, session state machine, transcript-writer golden, live-pane reducer, URL validation). The branch's only failing suites are in files it does not touch (pre-existing on `develop`).

## Headline: iOS on-device model download (#11841)
- **keep-awake during download** — reference-counted `keep_awake_set` that holds the iOS idle timer open while a model download is active, so auto-lock can't suspend the in-process transfer. Wired on **both** engine paths: the JSContext compat bridge *and* the full-Bun `host_call` dispatch (`FullBunEngineHost`), with the JavaScriptCore-free core split so it compiles into the full-Bun target. Verified on-device: BUILD SUCCEEDED + installed on an iPhone 16 Pro Max running the full-Bun engine; the `host_call` path is live (`host call method=…`). Downloader unit tests 20/20.
- **first-run download-trigger bug** — picking "On this device (recommended)" set `localInference=cloud-inference` because the inference-step parser's cloud regex matched the word **"recommended"**, so the local model download was never requested. Removed "recommended" from the cloud signal (cloud still matches on cloud/eliza cloud/online); added a regression test (first-run 22/22).
- **BootCaptureUITests** — added `holdForLocalModelDownload` (opt-in `ELIZA_LOCAL_MODEL_DOWNLOAD_WAIT_SECONDS`) so the local-onboarding capture keeps the app alive through the fire-and-forget download instead of tearing it down; harness knob added.

Outcomes are captured **as-is** — most scenarios *fail* on this smaller model tier (they were calibrated for the flagship with fully wired connectors; here connectors are mocked). No cherry-picking. 🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)22:T102a,## What & why

Follow-up to **#11789** (real-model LifeOps/PA benchmark score history), closed via **#12045**. That PR ran the LifeOpsBench **Hermes** adapter against local eliza-1 models and scored **0.000 — a confound, not capability**: the gemma-4 models emit correct tools+args in gemma-native `tool_code` syntax that the Hermes XML adapter can't parse, so `agent_actions` came back empty. Its closing comment named the open residual (tracked under parent **#10721**): *"a clean capability number needs either the native `eliza` adapter on a GPU-recovered host, or teaching the Hermes adapter to parse gemma-native tool calls."*

This PR delivers that clean number via a **third path that needs neither** — the LifeOpsBench **`cerebras-direct`** agent (native OpenAI function-calling) on **Cerebras `gemma-4-31b`**, `--mode static` (deterministic `state_hash` scoring — no judge, no mock), **120 scenarios across all 10 domains**.

### Result — pass@1 **13.3% (16/120)**, mean score 0.245

calendar 50% · mail 25% · travel 25% · reminders 17% · finance 8% · focus 8% · contacts/messages/health/sleep 0%.

Crucially, **`agent_actions` is populated and executed on every domain** (`CALENDAR_CREATE_EVENT`, `MESSAGE_SEND`, `ENTITY`, `HEALTH`, …) — **no parse artifact**, unlike #12045's empty-actions confound. The 0.000s are *genuine* failure modes (hand-reviewed): partial world state (0.3 near-misses), correct state but wrong answer text, and read-loops hitting `max_turns`. So this is a real capability signal: gemma-4-31b is strong on single-shot writes, weak on multi-step read-summarize-and-answer.

### Also operationalizes the retention decision #12045 made in prose

- **`score-history.jsonl` + `HISTORY.md`** — an append-only, harness-keyed series seeded with every real-model point to date: TS prompt-benchmark `gpt-oss-120b` 70% (#12022), local eliza-1 0.0% confounded (#12045), and this `cerebras-direct` 13.3% clean. Regenerated from the jsonl; never hand-edited.
- **`scripts/append-score-history.mjs`** — zero-dep appender ingesting either report format (`--report` or `--report-dir`).
- **`.github/workflows/lifeops-benchmark-history.yml`** — the nightly lane #12045's README named, **opt-in and inert on merge**: no-ops without a `CEREBRAS_API_KEY` secret, `schedule:` commented out. A maintainer enables it with the secret + cron.

## Evidence (`.github/issue-evidence/10721-lifeops-benchmark-history/lifeops-bench-cerebras-gemma-4-31b/`)

- **Real-LLM trajectories:** raw per-domain JSON (`lifeops_gemma-4-31b_<domain>.json`) with the full model I/O per turn — `agent_actions`, `agent_message`, tokens, latency, `total_score`, `state_hash_match` (the deterministic `tool_results` world observations are elided for size, reproducible via the run command; 356KB total); a hand-reviewed `scorecard.md`; `scorecard.sample-trajectories.json` (one pass + one fail); machine `scorecard.summary.json`. All from live `api.cerebras.ai` calls; liveness in `cerebras-endpoint-proof-gemma-4-31b.txt`.

# Issue 11903 Android boot evidence

Captured on 2026-07-03 from device `27051JEGR10034` using the debug APK at
`packages/app-core/platforms/android/app/build/outputs/apk/debug/app-debug.apk`.

## Build

- `bun run --cwd packages/agent build:mobile`
  - `agent-bundle.js`: 28.83 MB
  - `agent-deferred.js`: 15.80 MB
  - Mobile load smoke passed, including deferred plugin registration into the
    shared static registry after importing both bundle graphs.
- `ANDROID_SERIAL=27051JEGR10034 ELIZA_MOBILE_REPO_ROOT=/tmp/eliza-11903-android-boot ELIZA_WEBVIEW_DEBUG=1 ELIZA_BUN_RISCV64_OPTIONAL=1 ELIZA_ANDROID_SKIP_FORK_LLAMA_LIB=1 bun run --cwd packages/app build:android`
  - APK build and artifact audit passed.
- `ANDROID_SERIAL=27051JEGR10034 bun run --cwd packages/app install:android:adb -- --apk /tmp/eliza-11903-android-boot/packages/app-core/platforms/android/app/build/outputs/apk/debug/app-debug.apk`
  - On-device APK SHA matched the local APK (`668672175c48...`).
  - A fresh launch recapture from this final APK was blocked because the device
    was `RUNNING_LOCKED`; Android would install and parse the APK, but would not
    resolve/start non-direct-boot-aware activities or services until unlock.

## Device boot capture

- `boot-timing.txt`: forwarded `/api/health` returned ready after 20,166 ms.
- `health-ready.json`: backend reported `ready: true`, `runtime: ok`, `database: ok`, `plugins.loaded: 9`, `plugins.failed: 0`, `agentState: running`.
- `logcat.txt` / `logcat-late.txt`: Android service and renderer logs, with local bearer tokens redacted.
- `device-final.png` / `device-final-late.png`: screenshots captured from the same run.

## Caveat

This APK was built with `ELIZA_ANDROID_SKIP_FORK_LLAMA_LIB=1` because the local
workspace did not have an Android arm64 fused inference library staged for
`:app:copyForkLlamaLib`. The capture proves the Android staged JS bundle
extracts, evaluates, binds the API, and reaches backend ready. It does not prove
full local model inference or final UI readiness: `/api/status` reports
`state: "running"` and `canRespond: false`, while the WebView eventually renders
the startup-error screen after native-bridge requests report local-agent
unavailable.

## Local validation

- Rebasing onto latest origin/develop completed cleanly.
- bun install completed successfully after the final rebase.
- Full bun run verify passed on the immediately prior rebased head; after develop moved again, a repeat full verify on dd7afecd82b18061381082c4990045de57262d80 was stopped after a TypeScript build I/O hang with no failures emitted.
- Focused checks on dd7afecd82b18061381082c4990045de57262d80 passed: audit:type-safety-ratchet, packages/agent build:mobile, packages/agent typecheck, packages/app-core mobile build tests, Atlas Cloud video test, packages/cloud/shared typecheck, plugin-meetings typecheck, plugin-aosp-local-inference typecheck.

Fixes #11903 by changing Android mobile agent boot from one monolithic bundle into a boot bundle plus `agent-deferred.js`, then tightening the boot graph so expensive optional code does not compile/evaluate before the local API is ready.

Key changes:
- Build and stage `agent-deferred.js` for Android/iOS mobile runtime assets.
- Load deferred core static plugins after API readiness in `packages/agent/src/runtime/eliza.ts`.
- Use Android runtime-only plugin subpaths for contacts/phone/wifi to keep React/UI/Three graph out of boot.
- Keep optional app-manager, streaming, agent-skills, orchestrator, and TypeScript compiler paths lazy/runtime-only.
- Add an opt-in `ELIZA_MOBILE_ANALYZE=1` mobile bundle metafile report.
- Fix host load-smoke asset placement for PGlite extension tarballs so the smoke matches Android extraction layout.

## Remaining caveat

The APK evidence is a smoke/local-backend proof, not full local-model proof. This workspace does not have the Android arm64 fused inference library staged, so the Android build required `ELIZA_ANDROID_SKIP_FORK_LLAMA_LIB=1`; without it, `:app:copyForkLlamaLib` fails before APK assembly. The final device capture proves the JS bundle extracts, evaluates, binds the API, and reaches backend ready. It does not prove full local inference or final UI readiness: `/api/status` reports `state: "running"` and `canRespond: false`, and the WebView eventually renders the startup-error screen after native-bridge requests report local-agent unavailable.28:T1459,## What & why

The launcher accumulated a multi-page state machine that was **production-dead**: `curateLauncherPages` always returns ≤1 page, so the inner grid pager, page dots, dual page state, and the `launcherPage`/`launcherPageCount` store fields never did anything. On top of that sat a self-described legacy window-event navigation bridge and a pile of set-but-never-read props/constants/telemetry. Phase 1 rips all of it out. **User-facing behavior is unchanged** — this is a pure slop/legacy/dead-state removal — so the safety net is the two real-browser walkthroughs staying green.

Part of #12179 (Phase 1 = state/prop cleanup, WI-1..WI-4). WI-3 (the nested-pager pointer-claim registry in `useHorizontalPager`) ships as a **stacked follow-up PR** on this branch.

### WI-1 — page-only shell store + kill the event bridge
- `shell-surface-store` state shrinks to `{ page }`: deleted `launcherPage`/`launcherPageCount` + setters + the clamp half of `normalize`, deleted `home-launcher-events.ts` + `ensureWindowBridge`/`bridgedWindow`, moved `HomeLauncherPage` into the store.
- `useShellController.navigateHome` calls `goHome()` directly; the dead `navigateToViews` controller method + its 4 e2e fixture stubs are removed. (The live `ViewStatusStates.navigateToViews` is a different symbol — untouched.)
- `HomeLauncherSurface` drops the never-passed `className` prop and the always-true `railButtonsEnabled` gate (`PagerEdgeButtons` renders unconditionally, self-hiding each chevron at the rail edges).

### WI-2 — single-page launcher
- `LauncherProps` → `{ entries, loading?, onLaunch, className? }`. Deleted `pageGroups`/`page`/`onPageChange`/`onPageCountChange`/`showPageDots`, the `localPage` dual mode, the inner `useHorizontalPager` + inner `PagerEdgeButtons` + page dots + the `byId` re-join.
- `curateLauncherPages` returns `ViewEntry[]`; dropped the dead `"rolodex"` from `LAUNCHER_HIDDEN_IDS` (the canonical dedup already collapses it into `relationships`).
- `LauncherSurface` passes curated entries straight through (`onLaunch={handleLaunch}`) and drops `page-swipe` telemetry emission.

### WI-4 — dead props / constants / write-only state / telemetry prune
- Removed `HomeScreen.clockAccessory` (whitelabel keeps the whole-component `homeScreen` boot-config seam).
- Deleted `state/launcher-layout.ts`; `LAUNCHER_PAGE_SIZE` had zero users, `LAUNCHER_DOCK_LIMIT` (=4) inlined into its only consumer `useDesktopTabs.ts`.
- Deleted `view-recents.ts` (written on every launch, never read) + both `recordRecentViewId` call sites.
- Pruned `ViewInteractionAction` to `launch | hero-image-error | conversation-swipe-jank` and dropped the unused `query` field.

## Frozen contracts preserved (unchanged)
`home-launcher-surface` + `data-page`, sr-only `home-launcher-page-probe`, `home-launcher-rail`, `home-launcher-{home,launcher}-page`, `launcher`, `launcher-page-window`, `launcher-tile-<id>`, `home-screen`, `home-notification-pull-zone`, `rail-pager-edge-{prev,next}`, iOS smoke waits in `packages/app/src/main.tsx`.

2b:T5d4,The iOS device build (`ELIZA_IOS_FULL_BUN_ENGINE=1`) fails to compile:

```
KeepAwakeBridge.swift:34:13: error: value of type 'JSContext' has no member 'installBridgeFunction'
** BUILD FAILED **  (xcodebuild exited with code 65)
```

#11897 split the JSContext `keep_awake_set` installer into `KeepAwakeBridge+JSContext.swift` specifically so the core `KeepAwakeBridge` type stays **JavaScriptCore-free** and compiles into the full-Bun engine build — whose podspec (`ElizaosCapacitorBunRuntime.podspec`, `include_full_bun_engine` branch) omits `JavaScriptCore` and lists only a minimal source set (no `JSContextHelpers.swift`, no `+JSContext.swift`). But the base `KeepAwakeBridge.swift` still carried a leftover `install(into ctx: JSContext)` (+ `import JavaScriptCore`) that calls `ctx.installBridgeFunction` — the extension defined in the excluded `JSContextHelpers.swift`. So the full-Bun build can't resolve it (and the compat build would hit a redeclaration vs the `+JSContext` extension).

Removed the duplicate installer + import from the core file. The JSContext install now lives solely in `KeepAwakeBridge+JSContext.swift` (compat build); the full-Bun path drives the same ref-counted holder via `setEnabled` from `FullBunEngineHost`.

Verified by re-running `ios:device:deploy` to a physical iPhone (build proceeds past the Swift compile). Native/on-device evidence attached once the device install completes.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)2c:Tbbb,## What

Epic #10724 requires *each shipped optimization to have a measured improvement*. This ships the ranked eager-boot lazy-load win: the **settings section component graph** was still on the first-paint critical path.

The section *bodies* were already `React.lazy` (#11351), but both eager boot barrels (`index.ts` = `@elizaos/ui`, `browser.ts` = `@elizaos/ui/browser`) re-exported the registry accessors (`getAllSettingsSections`, `getSettingsSection`, `listSettingsSections`, `registerSettingsSection`) **from the heavy `settings-sections.ts`** — even though those accessors live in the light `settings-section-registry.ts` (type-only imports, zero runtime deps). That re-export forced `settings-sections.ts` to evaluate at boot, dragging in ~20 lucide icon chunks, the eager cloud-connectors upsell components, and the built-in registration side effects.

## Fix

- Move `getAllSettingsSections` into the light `settings-section-registry.ts` (it is an alias of `listSettingsSections`).
- Repoint both barrels' re-exports to the light registry.
- `settings-sections.ts` now loads only when the already-lazy `SettingsView` imports it, where its registration side effects still run.

No component, registry data, or render logic changed — only the output chunk a module lands in. Render-neutral by construction.

## Measured improvement — `packages/app` production build, `bundle-kpi.mjs` (brotli)

| metric (brotli)                                | before    | after     | delta                  |
|------------------------------------------------|-----------|-----------|------------------------|
| **initial entry** (first-paint import closure) | 2552.0 KB | 2311.5 KB | **−240.4 KB (−9.4%)**  |
| chunks fetched on first paint                  | 321       | 263       | −58 files              |
| eager chunk count                              | 397       | 360       | −37 chunks             |

The `settings-sections` registration marker moved from the eager `main-*.js` chunk into the **lazy `SettingsView-*.js` chunk (37 KB)** — confirmed absent from `index.html` after the change. Honest note: the *total* eager set moved only −17.7 KB (bodies were already lazy); the real, boot-critical win is the −240 KB off the first-paint closure. Before/after JSON in evidence.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)2d:Ta88,Closes #10722.

## What & why

`plugins/plugin-xr/simulator` exposed `emulator.setHandPose()` with no spec
driving it into the 3D scene, and gaze (`targetRayMode: "gaze"`) had no test.
This finishes real, headless (IWER-backed, no headset) coverage for both.

### Hand tracking
- New `emulator.dragHand()` (+ fixture wrapper + typed API) — the hand-tracking
  analogue of `dragController()`: hand world ray → scene `hitTest` → `dragPanel`,
  entirely through the real scene bridge.
- New case in `simulator/e2e/hand-input.spec.ts`: a pinch-posed right hand grabs
  the settings panel and drags it +0.6 m along world +X. Asserts the **semantic
  outcome** — the panel's world pose actually changes, the un-grabbed wallet
  panel stays put, and the authored scene dispatches a real `move` SpatialAction
  with the new x. Same seam the controller drag uses.
- (Already on `develop`, kept green here) pinch-select fires the authored view's
  real press handler exactly once via the HAND `XRInputSource` — the same DOM
  handler the controller path fires.

### Gaze — honest about the emulator limit
IWER 2.2.1 **cannot** surface a live `targetRayMode: "gaze"` /
`"transient-pointer"` XRInputSource: both are enum-only, the only live sources
(controller + hand) are hard-coded to `tracked-pointer`, and
`XRDevice.primaryInputMode` only toggles controller↔hand. `ActionPlayer` can
replay a `gaze`-tagged recording but that bypasses the live input pipeline (no
gamepad transitions → no `select`), so it is not interactive gaze.

Therefore interactive gaze **selection** is not emulable and is **not** claimed
as tested — only head-gaze **aiming** is (real headset forward ray → computed
hit, flat + 3D). The blocker is pinned by an **executable assertion** in
`gaze-input.spec.ts` that fails if a future IWER gains gaze emulation. Coverage
notes in `plugin-xr` AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md were updated to state this plainly (no
doc claims gaze selection is tested).

`.github/issue-evidence/10722-xr-hand-gaze/` — real 3D panel screenshots +
per-frame pose/ray/hit JSON (e.g. hand-right `pinch`, unit ray dir, hit
`elementId: save`, `panelId: settings`, world point).

```

```

  `noEmptyPattern`/`noNonNullAssertion` in `playwright-fixture.ts` are on develop, untouched).

2f:T1489,Closes #11788 (parent: #10721)

## What

LifeOps calendar actions now honor RRULE/recurring-event semantics through the whole pipeline — action → shared contracts → CalendarService → plugin-google — instead of flattening recurring events into read-only instances:

- **Create**: `CreateLifeOpsCalendarEventRequest.recurrence` carries RFC 5545 lines to `events.insert`. The CALENDAR action extracts RRULEs from natural language ("every monday at 7am" → `RRULE:FREQ=WEEKLY;BYDAY=MO`), and structured callers can pass `details.recurrence` (alias `rrule`). Invalid rules **fail closed** (400 `CALENDAR_INVALID_RECURRENCE`) — never a silent one-off event.
- **Instance vs series**: mutations on a recurring occurrence take `recurrenceScope: "instance" | "series"`. Resolution is structural: explicit detail > deterministic phrasing ("just this one" / "the whole series") > LLM extraction > **clarification round-trip** (no mutation on ambiguity).
- **Series mutations target the master**: `scope=series` resolves the series-master id from the cached occurrence's `recurringEventId` (provider `getEvent` fallback) and issues **one** patch/delete — no iteration over flattened occurrences. Series delete purges every cached occurrence of that series while preserving other grants' rows (multi-account isolation).
- **Readback**: `recurrence` + `recurringEventId` are first-class on `GoogleCalendarEvent` and `LifeOpsCalendarEvent` (and round-trip through the PGlite cache via metadata — no schema migration).
- **Apple path fails closed**: recurring create/update/delete on Apple Calendar → 400 `CALENDAR_RECURRENCE_UNSUPPORTED_PROVIDER` (EventKit bridge has no recurrence support), never a silently-degraded one-off.
- **New module** `plugins/plugin-calendar/src/internal/recurrence.ts` (no rrule lib exists in the repo; Google expands server-side, so this owns only validation, DST-correct local expansion/next-occurrence for the DAILY/WEEKLY/MONTHLY/YEARLY subset with INTERVAL/BYDAY/BYMONTHDAY/COUNT/UNTIL, and human-readable descriptions). Expansion iterates local wall-clock parts on the timezone-safe primitives in `internal/time.ts` — one fire per local day across DST transitions (same care as the scheduled-task cron DST fix).
- Routes/client: PATCH body + `DELETE ?recurrenceScope=` passthrough; `deleteLifeOpsCalendarEvent` client exposes `recurrenceScope`.

LifeOps frozen contracts respected: no new scheduler, no promptInstructions-driven behavior — scope/recurrence are typed structural fields; the only prompt changes are extraction field additions.

## Tests (all local — CI rarely completes; local run is the gate)

- **54 new tests**: `recurrence.test.ts` (25 — parse/normalize fail-closed, DST spring-forward + fall-back expansion with hand-computed instants independently verified against `Intl`, COUNT/UNTIL termination, weekly/monthly next-occurrence, negative BYMONTHDAY, leap-year YEARLY), `calendar-recurrence-ops.test.ts` (14 — handler instance-vs-series guardrails incl. ambiguity clarification with zero mutation calls), `calendar-recurrence-service.test.ts` (13 — real PGlite cache + spied provider seam: master resolution, series purge with grant isolation, Apple rejection), 2 route-passthrough tests, 1 plugin-google connector contract test (insert/patch `requestBody.recurrence`, readback mapping).
- **Fail-without-fix**: the new suites run against pre-fix `calendar-handler.ts` + `CalendarService.ts` → **22 failures** (`.github/issue-evidence/10721-calendar-rrule/fail-without-fix.txt`).
- Full suites green: plugin-calendar **195 passed / 2 skipped**, plugin-google **22**, packages/shared **1049**. `turbo typecheck` across plugin-calendar/plugin-google/shared/plugin-personal-assistant/plugin-health/ui/app-core: **92/92 tasks**. Biome clean on touched files (3 remaining warnings pre-exist on develop).

## Evidence (`.github/issue-evidence/10721-calendar-rrule/`)

**Live-LLM trajectory** (`live-llm-trajectory.json`, reviewed by hand): the production CALENDAR handler driven by a **live model** (claude CLI, haiku) at the `CalendarActionDeps` seam with a spied service —

1. *"book a 30 minute morning run … every monday at 7am eastern"* → model extracted `RRULE:FREQ=WEEKLY;BYDAY=MO`; `createCalendarEvent` received it; reply: *Created calendar event "morning run" … **It repeats weekly on Monday.***
2. *"move my team standup to 10am"* (recurring occurrence) → **clarification, no mutation**: *"Team Standup" repeats weekly on Wednesday. should i change just this occurrence or the whole series?*
3. *"move just this one team standup occurrence to 10am"* → `updateCalendarEvent` with occurrence id + `recurrenceScope:"instance"`.
4. *"delete the whole series of my team standup"* → exactly **one** `deleteCalendarEvent` with `recurrenceScope:"series"`.

34:T1214,Closes part of #11373 (follow-up to #9583). Brings the on-device AEC acoustic-loop harness up on **real mobile devices** and fixes the on-device bugs that made an on-device capture impossible. Full evidence + honest blockers below.

## What this fixes (all found by running the harness on a real device)

Before this PR the #9583 harness could not capture on **any** mobile device:

1. **Route gate** (`packages/agent/src/api/server.ts`) — the mobile agent only dispatched `/api/voice/audio-frames*` to `handleLiveDiarizationRoute`, so `/api/voice/playback-frames` (the live playback-reference producer the acceptance criteria require) **and** `/api/voice/aec-capture` both **404'd** on-device. Added both exact paths.
2. **Android fused-lib env** (`ElizaAgentService.java`) — nothing exported `ELIZA_INFERENCE_LIBRARY`, so `/api/voice/audio-frames` **500'd** with "fused libelizainference not found" even though `libelizainference.so` ships in the APK. Now exported (guarded on the `.so` shipping). **Verified on emulator-5554 via logcat.**
3. **AEC decoupled from the native diarizer** (`live-diarization-session.ts`) — `ingest()` threw the whole transport when the fused voice lib is absent (**iOS embeds only `ElizaBunEngine`, no fused voice lib**). The AEC seam (delay calibration + near/far capture) is pure-TS and is the actual subject of #9583, so the fused consumer is now best-effort. New echo test covers the lib-absent path.
4. **Bridge-free retrieval** (`live-diarization-route.ts`) — `GET /api/voice/aec-capture` also mirrors the near/far PCM + counters to `$ELIZA_STATE_DIR/eliza-aec-capture.json` (the WebView Capacitor Filesystem sink can't land multi-MB results on iOS). Unit-tested.
5. **Harness/driver** — `audioUrl` far-end for TTS-less devices; iOS deep-link via `devicectl --payload-url` (plain `process launch <url>` mis-parses the URL); harness tolerates a ~2.5-min cold-DB agent boot (was 90 s).

## Evidence

- **Android emulator app-path (real)** — `.github/issue-evidence/9583-aec-android-emulator/`: `playbackSamplesReceived=417600`, `echoReferenceWired=true`, `libelizainference.so` loaded (logcat), all three `/api/voice/*` routes reachable, app running screenshot. Acoustic ERLE is 0 dB because the emulator's **virtual mic is a synthetic/saturated source, not the host mic** (`mic-characterization.json`: 0.67 RMS / max 1.0 with host output muted vs a 0.021 host-mic floor) — an emulator I/O limit, not an app defect.
- **iOS physical device (MoonCycles, iPhone 16 Pro Max)** — `.github/issue-evidence/9583-aec-ios-device/`: build → sign → install → **boot to agent-ready** trace, and the deep-link URL delivered to the app (`UIOpenURLAction`). See the blocker below.
- **macOS real acoustic loop** (already accepted, same production canceller) — `.github/issue-evidence/9583-aec-macos/`: ~68 ms delay, full ERLE series, synthetic-echo validity control.

## Honest blockers (why the physical-iPhone acoustic ERLE row is not filled)

On a **cold-launched** physical iPhone the deep-link URL reaches the app natively (`UIOpenURLAction`), but the harness never executes — an agent-side "armed" probe confirmed the harness's first call never lands, so the Capacitor cold-launch `appUrlOpen → handleDeepLink → #aec-loop` hand-off does not fire. Reliably triggering it needs either that hand-off to work or **Web Inspector/CDP** (a human must enable *Settings → Safari → Advanced → Web Inspector*). Even once triggered, an autonomous run is gated by two **iOS OS-level human taps** — screen **unlock** and the **microphone permission** prompt (iOS has no CLI mic-grant, unlike Android's `pm grant`). None are clearable headless. Details + boot trace in the iOS README.

## Verification

- `bunx vitest run` green for the touched voice suites (harness parse, echo/decoupling, route incl. persistence, echo-delay seeds): **53 + 20 tests pass**.
- biome clean on changed files.
- Android fixes exercised on a live `emulator-5554` app-path run; iOS fixes exercised on a live device up to the trigger.

## N/A rows (with reason)

- **Physical-device acoustic ERLE / double-talk / measured delay** — blocked by the iOS trigger + unlock + mic-permission human gates above; not faked. The Pixel 6a called for in #9583 is not attached (delegate to hardware-lab #11734).
- **Delay-constant update** — the seeds in `echo-delay.ts` are unchanged: no *new* clean acoustic device measurement was obtainable this session (emulator mic synthetic; iPhone gated). The macOS ~68 ms measurement already exists.

35:T873b,## Summary
- Export the existing tags-first coding delegation resolver from core and reuse it outside `services/message`.
- Classify automation runtime action nodes by declared orchestration/delegation tags instead of stale `START_CODING_TASK` / `CREATE_TASK` / `CODE_TASK` names.
- Route plugin-manager, app-control, and coding-tools delegation dispatch through the shared resolver so the current `TASKS` action works via tags/similes.
- Route Telegram setup deep-link delivery through the runtime send-handler registry instead of core reaching into plugin-telegram internals.
- Resolve Discord inbox enrichment through plugin-discord's service constant and service type instead of a raw `"discord"` lookup plus inline duck type.
- Replace raw agent chat event emits with `EventType` constants and drop the `MODEL_USED` string hedge.
- Reuse the shared linked-account provider guard in account-pool persistence instead of a duplicated provider-id list.
- Export and consume `AUTONOMY_SERVICE_TYPE` for agent/app-core autonomy service lookup and registration; app-core keeps the lowercase fallback as an explicit legacy shim.
- Add a core `FOLLOW_UP_CAPABLE_ACTION_TAG`; `CONTACT` and `OWNER_REMINDERS` declare it, and the core ACTIONS provider filters relationship follow-up turns by tag instead of downstream action names.
- Move the tunnel service slot to core (`ServiceType.TUNNEL`, `ITunnelService`, `TunnelStatus`, `getTunnelService`) and update core/app-core/plugin-tunnel/plugin-tailscale consumers to use the shared contract.
- Move the cloud-auth service slot to core (`ServiceType.CLOUD_AUTH`, `CLOUD_AUTH_SERVICE_TYPE`, `ICloudAuthService`, `getCloudAuthService`) and update the relationships graph builder plus plugin-elizacloud registration to use the shared contract.
- Reuse core-owned sub-agent credential bridge/dispatch service constants in app-core and gate parent bridge wiring through a shared core capability marker instead of app-core-owned ACP service strings.
- Type the Binance direct-skill fast path against exported core/agent-skills action and service contracts instead of raw `USE_SKILL` / `AGENT_SKILLS_SERVICE` strings and local duck types.
- Key app-core PGlite startup recovery on plugin-sql exported `PGLITE_ERROR_CODES` instead of an app-core-owned reset-code literal; remaining message sniffing is marked as legacy/unstructured fallback.
- Centralize the Capacitor native `Agent` plugin contract in `packages/ui/src/bridge/native-plugins.ts` and update first-run local-agent token/probe/start paths to use `getAgentPlugin()`.

37:T4e80,Part of #12089.

## What changed

This PR completes a focused self-registration/initialization slice from the #12089 punchlist:

- Item 1 / P0: action role gates are now derived from the per-runtime context registry, so plugin-registered contexts participate in planned-tool-call authorization. The default-only `DEFAULT_CONTEXT_ROLE_BY_ID` snapshot is removed.
- Item 6 / P1: cloud TTS preview is now owned by `@elizaos/plugin-elizacloud:routes` through `Plugin.routes`; app-core no longer dynamically imports `@elizaos/plugin-elizacloud` or hardcodes provider-specific `/api/tts/cloud` and `/api/tts/elevenlabs` dispatcher branches.
- Item 10 / P1: website-blocking permission ownership now flows through `PermissionRegistry.registerProber`. The agent permission routes no longer dynamically import `@elizaos/plugin-personal-assistant` or special-case the `website-blocking` id; LifeOps registers the real SelfControl prober, including an `openSettings` hook, when its plugin initializes.
- Item 13 / P2: the lazy Cloud route bridge now shares typed cloud handler contracts across `server-route-dispatch.ts`, `external-modules.d.ts`, and the agent public lazy re-exports. The `/api/cloud/*` dispatch path no longer uses erased `unknown[]` handler signatures or the `cloudState as never` cast.
- Item 14 / P2: the mobile model-provider allow-list now lives in the shared `core-plugins.ts` contract as `MOBILE_MODEL_PROVIDER_PLUGINS`; `plugin-collector.ts` consumes it instead of duplicating provider package names inline.
- Item 16 / P2: `ViewDeclaration` now carries plugin-owned `relatedActions`/`contextHints`, and view-scoped action affinity is derived from the live view registry instead of the agent trunk `VIEW_ACTION_MAP`. Plugin and built-in view declarations now own their action affinity metadata.
- Item 17 / P2: app-core credential resolution now derives API-key sources from core `MODEL_PROVIDER_SECRETS` + `SECRET_KEY_ALIASES`, normalizes request aliases through first-run provider metadata, and resolves direct-account providers through agent/core provider declarations instead of local hand-maintained maps.
- Item 20 / P2: removed app-core's `agent-orchestrator -> edge-tts` plugin-name injection from `collectPluginNames`. `@elizaos/plugin-agent-orchestrator` now declares `@elizaos/plugin-edge-tts` through `Plugin.dependencies`, while `ensureTextToSpeechHandler` remains the runtime model-registration fallback.
- Item 23 / P2: default TTS provider registry resolution is lazy. Importing `tts-provider-registry.ts` no longer throws if the generated `edge-tts` registry entry is missing; strict failure now happens only when TTS registration/use asks for the provider.
- Item 24 / P2: connector target enumeration now flows through core `TargetSourceRegistryService` registrations. App-core drains registered `TargetSource`s for the connector-target catalog, and Discord owns its guild/channel enumerator registration inside `plugin-discord` instead of app-core hardcoding Discord platform logic.
- Item 25 / P2: trigger-event passive connector suppression now reads connector-source metadata (`isPassive`/`sourceKind`) from the core connector source registry, including aliases such as `discord-local` and `x_dm`; the app-core trigger bridge no longer owns a passive source-name set.
- Item 26 / P2: connector source aliases now live on plugin-owned `Plugin.connectorSources` declarations and are registered during plugin registration; core/shared no longer carry a hardcoded connector alias map or duplicate connector metadata implementation.
- Item 27 / P2: control-message delivery now resolves a typed `ServiceType.CONTROL_TRANSPORT` service through `runtime.getService` instead of scanning registered service names for websocket/socket substrings and duck-typing `sendMessage`.
- Item 28 / P2: the shared UI navigation bus now supports generic one-shot `payload` handoff keyed by `viewId`. `app-navigate-view.ts` no longer contains Phone/Messages-specific pending state or helper functions; Contacts dispatches generic payloads, while Phone and Messages consume typed payloads from the generic API.
- Item 29 / P2: overlay apps, app-shell pages, and settings sections now use one shared UI registry host instead of per-registry `window.__elizaos*` or `Symbol.for` storage slots. Tests reset the shared host directly rather than reaching into globals.
- Item 33 / P2: app-shell page registrations now declare `tabAffinity` when a route should activate an existing shell tab. `tabFromPath` consumes the registration metadata and the hardcoded wallet/phone-companion reverse alias maps are removed from the navigation trunk.
- Item 36 / P3: removed the redundant `@elizaos/plugin-personal-assistant -> @elizaos/plugin-google` boot dependency from the agent host; the plugin already declares that dependency itself.
- Item 37 / P3: removed the Vercel AI Gateway cwd-relative filesystem probe from the generic plugin collector. Env-driven provider intent is now collected uniformly and missing optional packages are handled by the resolver's existing benign-unavailable path.
- Item 38 / P3: moved WeChat channel ownership into `plugins/plugin-wechat/registry-entry.json`, regenerated the first-party channel map, and removed the app-core/shared hardcoded `elizaoswechat` overrides.
- Item 39 / P3: replaced duplicated `INTENTIONAL_MULTI_SERVICE_TYPES` sets with a `Service.allowsMultiple` declaration on service classes. Wallet, LP pool, token data, and trajectory services now declare the policy at the owning service definition.
- Item 40 / P3: moved native-feature service-type mapping into `plugins/native-features.ts` as a typed registry and made runtime lookup derive from it. Rebased over develop's advancedPlanning/advancedMemory registry move with empty service lists for those non-service gates.
- Item 42 / P3: removed runtime-owned `web_search` search-category fabrication. `plugin-web-search` owns category registration through `registerSearchCategory` during plugin init, with regression coverage on both the runtime and plugin sides.
- Item 43 / P3: removed dead static component re-exports and sync comments from `packages/ui/src/app-shell-components.ts`, keeping only app-shell registry exports.
- Rebase unblocker: after syncing with latest `develop`, removed stale trust provider barrel exports for files deleted by #12103 so `packages/core` typecheck passes again.
- Rebase unblocker: typed `publicReason` through the worker-runtime plugin shape so remote public route descriptors remain type-safe after the latest `develop` route-auth contract.

Additional Item 22 groundwork: `Route.modes` and `modeReason` can now be declared on plugin route registrations, and app-core's mode guard prefers registered runtime route visibility before falling back to the legacy matrix. The Cloud TTS preview route now owns its local/cloud/remote visibility in `@elizaos/plugin-elizacloud:routes`; the legacy matrix entry for `/api/tts/cloud` is removed. Remaining Item 22 work is to migrate legacy app-core-owned cloud compat/billing and local-inference compat surfaces after the compat-route registry lands.

39:T1373,## What

Captures the **four adb-measurable benchmark rows** of #11734 on the physical Pixel 6a (serial 27051JEGR10034, policy build `35f61d1f` — the same install the #11908 acceptance soak passed on), eliza-1-2b Q4 through the in-process bionic Vulkan host, driven over `adb forward tcp:31337`. The issue parked these rows as hardware-lab-gated; only **battery/power draw** (tethered = charging; discharge unmeasurable) and **iOS** genuinely are.

Full methodology + raw data: `.github/issue-evidence/11734-pixel6a-adb-rows/` (per-turn JSONL, session logcat, thermal TSV, memory timelines, analyzers). Non-gating `measuredBaselines` in `packages/benchmarks/mobile-resource/budgets.json` + `BASELINE.md` updated; gating `deviceClasses` budgets stay `null` per the multi-run-stability rule.

## The four rows

### 1. TTFT distribution (was: single warm run)
12 identical-length turns: **p50 54.3 s / p90 57.7 s** (min 52.9, max 58.8). Warm-only p50 54.3 / p90 54.5; 3/12 turns were transparent post-memory-pressure-release reloads (57.3–58.8 s) — deliberately included, that's what this RAM class delivers. Client-side overhead vs the native window is only ~136 ms. **Client TTFT = full-turn latency on this build**: the fast path emits the entire reply as one SSE chunk because the bionic `stream_next` decodes its full 256-token buffer in a single native call (`maxTokens=20` never engages; every ~9-token reply pays a ~46 s 256-token decode).

### 2. Isolated prefill tok/s (was: combined-decode only)
8-rung exact-token prompt ladder (92→219 Qwen3.5 tokens), regressing the device-logged in-lock work window (queue- and load-immune; verified against an unqueued turn's wall to 30 ms): `W = 32.4 s + 0.1945 s/tok × P` (r² 0.78) → **marginal prefill ≈ 5.1 tok/s**, effective decode ≤ 7.9 tok/s. Prefill is **batch-quantized** (`ELIZA_LLM_N_BATCH=128`): flat ≤127 prompt tokens (~53.7 s, decode-dominated), then ~+23 s per extra 128-token batch. Independent cross-check on the identical GGUF + Vulkan lib family with the app stopped: llama-bench **pp128 3.88 / pp512 8.86 / tg128 6.78 tok/s**. The historical "4.8 tok/s warm decode" baseline is a combined-window rate, now documented as such.

### 3. Thermal timeline (was: point snapshot)
91.4 min continuous session, 15 s cadence, 363 samples (`dumpsys thermalservice` HAL + battery; `/sys/class/thermal` is permission-blocked on this user build). **Thermal Status stayed 0 the whole session** — the OS throttling ladder never engaged under sustained ~55 s/turn inference from a cool start. Peaks: skin_therm1 42.9 °C, VIRTUAL-SKIN 38.1 °C, charger_skin 47.9 °C, TPU 68 °C, battery 29.2 °C. Bonus bound for the battery row: level fell **76%→70% while USB-powered** (sustained inference outdraws the adb supply), then recharged.

### 4. eliza-1-4b tier (was: not captured) — the kill IS the measurement
Staged the published device-class 4b (`bundles/4b/text/eliza-1-4b-256k.gguf`, 2.95 GB Q4_K_M, sha256-verified), switched registry+assignments, relaunched, drove one turn with 5 s memory sampling. Result, exactly as the E2B-4.9GB finding predicted: **lowmemorykiller killed the foreground app during the load** — `ApplicationExitInfo reason=3 (LOW_MEMORY), rss=3.4GB, importance=100`; GL mtrack 3.22 GB / PSS 3.51 GB at the last sample; MemAvailable 1670→206 MB in 22 s; kill 24 s after boot; Android auto-restart would retry the load (kill loop). The #11760 release policy cannot fire mid-load (the load holds the resident lock). The catalog's `minRamGb: 6` floor for this tier is confirmed real on this 5.59 GB device. The 2b was restored and verified with a real turn; the device ends in its original state.

## Additional findings (documented in the evidence README + budgets findings)

- **The shipped device-tier eliza-1 text GGUFs are Qwen3.5, not Gemma-4**: on-device `eliza-1-2b-q4.gguf` reports `general.architecture=qwen35` (base `Qwen/Qwen3.5-2B-Base`); same for the published 2b/4b `-256k`/`-64k` artifacts — only the 8 GB `-128k` files carry the Gemma-4 cutover. The fast path's Gemma-format template tags are plain text to this model, so decode always fills the 256-token native buffer.
- **An autonomous ~5-min background agent job** (11 k-char prompt, `maxTokens=8192`) takes longer than its own period at device prefill speed, so it self-queues and holds the resident-model lock indefinitely, starving chat turns — this poisoned two sweep attempts (kept in evidence) and forced the per-rung relaunch methodology.

## Still genuinely lab-gated (issue stays open for these)

- Battery / power draw (idle / chat / voice / background) — needs a bench power meter at the battery/USB terminals.
- iOS rows for both tiers — needs a physical iOS device.
- Multi-run stability (≥3 quiet, cool runs) before promoting anything into gating `deviceClasses` budgets, and the workflow-lane gate flip.

Refs #11734

Lands a **judge-graded GEPA scoring lane** for the 4 prose/NL LifeOps
capabilities — `reminder_dispatch`, `meeting_prep`, `morning_brief`,

slice of #11384.

  rubric-judge scorer (`createLifeOpsJudgeCompare`): grades a completion as the

  async so the judge comparator can grade with a live model (6-line change).

## Why

completions, so GEPA had **no gradient** to climb and could never improve the 4
prose capabilities. A rubric-judge produces a real continuous score.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)

https://claude.ai/code/session_01CwCMi6JqtSKTrsJuxry7gm
3b:T1bf9,## What

Track A of the conversation-context importer (#11881): a **new package `packages/import-conversations/`** shipping the **shared core** — normalized types + the ingestion pipeline. This is the foundation Tracks B/C/D (ChatGPT / Claude / Hermes parsers) build against, so it lands as a clean, well-tested contract with **no parsers** in it yet.

## Why

eliza starts cold. A user with years of ChatGPT / Claude / Hermes history has thousands of messages of preferences, projects, and context that today never make it in. The openclaw `migrate-claude` / `migrate-hermes` providers move agent *state* but explicitly archive conversation history without parsing it — that's the gap. This core lets normalized conversations land as searchable, scoped, deduped, uninstallable knowledge through eliza's existing `DocumentService`, without core depending on the runtime (so it's fully unit-testable).

Design decisions match the scoping doc (`conversation-importer-scope.md`, §3.4 model / §4 ingestion / §5 architecture):
- **Documents-first** (not per-message Memory rows) — free dedup, chunking, embedding, hybrid search, clean uninstall.
- **DocumentSink port** — core stores through a minimal `addDocument`/`deleteDocument` interface; the eliza-app side provides the thin `DocumentService` adapter. Core never imports the runtime.
- **Streaming by construction** — the pipeline consumes the parser's `AsyncIterable<NormalizedConversation>` and never buffers a full export.

## Per-module summary

- **`core/types.ts`** — `ConversationBundle` / `NormalizedConversation` / `NormalizedMessage` (scope §3.4). The contract B/C/D map into. Open `ConversationSource` string union for future sources.
- **`core/redact.ts`** — `redactText(s)` secret-pattern scrub. Patterns ported from ocplatform's migration SDK (`SECRET_VALUE_PATTERNS`: Bearer, `sk-`, `gh[pousr]_`, `xox[abprs]-`, `AIza…`) + added AWS access-key ids (`AKIA/ASIA`) and PEM private-key blocks, since pasted transcripts carry those. `runtime.createMemory` only redacts *configured* secrets, so the importer must scrub pasted ones itself.
- **`core/render.ts`** — `NormalizedConversation → markdown transcript` (§4.1: `# title (imported from Source, date)` header; `**role** (ts): text` lines; quoted attachment blocks). Splits long conversations (>~8k rendered tokens) at message boundaries with a configurable 1-2 message overlap, returning ordered part-documents. Guarantees forward progress even for single oversized messages.
- **`core/manifest.ts`** — import-batch state keyed by `(source, sourceConversationId, updatedAt)`. `added` / `unchanged` / `updated` classification, immutable `recordConversation`, and `enumerateBatchDocumentIds` for **full-batch uninstall** (hard requirement, §4.4).
- **`core/report.ts`** — plan/apply summary (`added/unchanged/updated/skipped/errors` + docs stored) with per-item reasons and a `notImported()` "what was NOT imported and why" view, mirroring openclaw's plan/apply discipline.
- **`core/registry.ts`** — `registerConversationImporter(parser)` — eliza's missing `registerMigrationProvider`-equivalent, scoped to conversation import. A parser is `{ source; detect(input); parse(input): AsyncIterable<NormalizedConversation> }`. Register/lookup/detect(first-match)/unregister.
- **`core/sink.ts`** — the `DocumentSink` port (`addDocument`→`{id,status}` / `deleteDocument`) plus scope/provenance value types aligned to `DocumentsServiceLike`.
- **`core/pipeline.ts`** — orchestrates **parse → redact → render → dedup(manifest) → store → report**. Applies scope (`user-private`), provenance (`addedFrom: "import"`, import metadata block, `import` / `import:<source>` / `conv:<id>` / `date:YYYY-MM` tags per §4.2/§4.4). Yields `{ processed, total }` progress events (background-job friendly). `updated` deletes prior docs then re-stores; sink-level content dedup on an `added` conversation is reported as a dedup skip. `uninstallBatch` + dry-run supported.

## TEST

New package, so there's no prior regression baseline to protect. **58 new unit tests, all passing** (fixture-driven, with a fake `DocumentSink` + fake streaming parser — no runtime/DB needed):

- **redact (15):** each secret pattern individually (sk-, sk-proj-, ghp_/gho_/ghs_, Bearer, xox*, AIza, AKIA/ASIA, PEM block), multiple-in-one-string, clean-text untouched, empty input, global-regex `lastIndex` stability, `containsSecret`.
- **render (10):** header (with/without date, Untitled fallback), message format (role+ts, no-ts, attachment-only empty text, image placeholder), single-part short conversation, long-split with overlap (overlap invariant + full coverage + part suffix), forward-progress on oversized messages, empty conversation header-only part.
- **manifest (9):** key namespacing, classify added/unchanged/updated/missing-updatedAt, immutability of record, documentIds overwrite on update, uninstall enumeration across batch, empty-entry lookup.
- **report (5):** count tally across all outcomes, dryRun flag, auto unchanged-reason, `notImported` filter, compact one-liner.
- **registry (7):** register/lookup, empty-source rejection, unregister, no-clobber-after-replace, detect first-match in order, async detect, clear.
- **pipeline (12):** end-to-end parse→store→report **with progress events** + provenance/scoping assertions, secret redaction before store, no-message skip, idempotent re-import (unchanged), newer-updatedAt re-import (updated: old docs deleted + new stored), sink content-dedup skip, dry-run (no store / no sink required / throws without sink on apply), batch uninstall, **streaming laziness** (one-ahead pull, incremental consumption of a lazy generator without materializing).

**Baseline-delta:** N/A prior baseline (new package). +58 new tests, 58 passing / 0 failing.

## Monorepo placement

### Product bug found by the measurement → fixed here

`ECHO_CAL_MAX_LAG_SAMPLES` was **4 800 samples (300 ms)** — below the real Pixel 6a delay. The one-shot self-calibration locked a **cap-edge** lag (~298 ms @ conf 0.32) and permanently pinned an ~85 ms misalignment; with a 256-tap (16 ms) filter the NLMS then **diverges** instead of cancelling. Fix (`live-diarization-session.ts`): ceiling → **8 000 (500 ms)**, **cap-edge locks rejected** (keep observing), calibration window 0.75 s → 1 s. Unit tests: calibrates a 400 ms delay; refuses a cap-edge lock. Verified on-device: post-fix locks land in-range (5 479 = 342 ms).

Remaining accuracy gap documented honestly: even in-range one-shot locks sit ~45 ms off the whole-run optimum because the delay itself drifts — robust Android AEC needs continuous delay tracking or the hardware-timestamped JNI far-end tap (#11562/#11372). That is follow-up engineering, quantified here with committed tools.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)3e:T20f4,Closes #11760.

## Policy chosen

**RAM-class-scoped footprint caps + idle unload + pressure-reactive release of the resident inference state — in the existing processes.** No `android:process` isolation.

The #11506 forensics show lmkd killing `ai.elizaos.app` at ~3.2 GB RSS on the 5.7 GB Pixel 6a because the resident inference state (model weights + KV cache + compute buffers — 2.25 GB of GL mtrack on the Vulkan host, ~1.2 GB of anon heap on the CPU path) is pinned for the life of the process, keeping the app the largest resident process at all times. Three levers fix that root cause directly:

1. **RAM-class defaults (issue ask b/d).** A single classifier — `CONSTRAINED` below 7 GiB usable (Pixel 6a reports 5.7 GiB; 8 GB-nominal devices report ≥7.3 GiB) — drives the resident LLM context: `ELIZA_LLM_N_CTX` 4096 (constrained) vs 8192 (standard) on the bionic host, and the branded in-process default drops 16384→8192 on constrained devices. `ELIZA_LLM_N_BATCH=128` (the compute-buffer lever) was already in place from the 8 GB-Mali work and is kept. Model tier needs no change: 2B Q4 is already both the catalog floor and the hardcoded mobile default.
2. **Idle unload (issue ask a).** The resident model + context + stream are freed after a RAM-class idle window — 5 min constrained / 30 min standard (`debug.eliza.inference.idle_unload_ms` / `ELIZA_LOCAL_IDLE_UNLOAD_MS` override; `0` disables). Reload cost is the documented ~7-8 s resident rebuild (`ensureResidentCtx`) or the in-process lifecycle reload (`ensureChatLoaded`) — both pre-existing, both lossless (agent/app state lives outside the inference context; per-turn KV is re-prefilled anyway).
3. **Pressure release (issue ask c + the acceptance OR-arm).** 
   - App process (bionic host): `onTrimMemory` relay (`RUNNING_LOW`/`RUNNING_CRITICAL`/`BACKGROUND`+) + a 30 s `ActivityManager.getMemoryInfo()` poll that releases within 512 MiB of the lmkd threshold on constrained devices (trim callbacks are deprecated/unreliable on API 34+; the poll is the dependable path).
   - Bun agent (in-process leg): the agent process never receives `onTrimMemory`, so `/proc/meminfo` `MemAvailable` is its pressure signal — release below 12 % available (constrained) / 5 % (standard).

**Why not process isolation:** the Vulkan inference already runs in the only process that can reach the Mali driver (`ElizaBionicInferenceServer`, in-app JNI — the musl bun agent's linker namespace cannot load the Vulkan HIDL closure). A separate `:inference` process would duplicate the JNI host + UDS plumbing, add its own overhead, and complicate the #11738-hardened service lifecycle — while the reclaim semantics it buys ("inference memory dies, app survives") are exactly what explicit release already delivers: `resetResident()` frees the GL mtrack deterministically and the next turn reloads. If post-policy field data still shows lmkd taking the app while a session is actively hot, isolation is the documented escalation; it is not needed to meet the acceptance.

The #11738 FGS-restart behavior is untouched (no changes to the restart/denial paths; `onTrimMemory` is additive).

## Implementation

**Android (app process):**
- `InferenceMemoryPolicy.java` (new) — pure decision logic: RAM-class classification (+ `debug.eliza.inference.ram_class` override for emulator verification), per-class n_ctx + idle windows, trim-level decisions, availMem-margin decisions. 17 JVM unit tests (`InferenceMemoryPolicyTest.java`, first tests in `:app:testDebugUnitTest`).
- `ElizaBionicInferenceServer.java` — RAM-class `ELIZA_LLM_N_CTX` default; single daemon scheduler (30 s tick) for idle + pressure release; `releaseResident(reason)` frees the resident model/ctx/stream under `residentLock` (an in-flight decode always finishes its turn first; the idle window is re-checked under the lock); loud structured release logs.
- `ElizaAgentService.java` — `onTrimMemory` relay (async; never blocks the main thread), `ActivityManager`-backed pressure probe, policy-decision log, and RAM-class env export to the bun agent (`ELIZA_INFERENCE_RAM_CLASS`, `ELIZA_LOCAL_IDLE_UNLOAD_MS`), constrained-branded n_ctx 8192.

**Bun agent (in-process AOSP leg):**
- `plugins/plugin-aosp-local-inference/src/inference-memory-policy.ts` (new) — mirrored classifier (env → `/proc/meminfo`), idle resolution, `MemAvailable` pressure check, and `InferenceIdleUnloader` (in-flight-aware; never unloads mid-generate).
- `aosp-local-inference-bootstrap.ts` — every loader call routes through idle tracking; unload flows through the existing `unloadModel` + `markEvicted` → `ensureChatLoaded` reload plumbing (same path the voice handlers' out-of-band eviction already uses). 24 policy tests + full plugin suite green (99).

**Bug found + fixed by the on-device verification:** the in-process AOSP chat load still requested the retired QJL/PolarQuant KV types (`qjl1_256`/`q4_polar`). Current fused libs route those into the stock llama.cpp V-quant check, which hard-fails without flash-attn (deliberately off on Android — the scalar-FA race), so **every in-process generate died with `llm_stream_open: failed to init llama context`** on the stock debug APK. Chat KV is now the Gemma path — `q8_0` K (halves the K cache, no FA needed, matches `ELIZA_1_KV_QUANT`) / `f16` V — with `ELIZA_LLAMA_CACHE_TYPE_K/V` env overrides.

**Gate (issue ask 3):**
- `packages/benchmarks/mobile-resource`: new `idle-reclaim` workload (runs last, after the chat workloads leave the model warm) + `maxPostIdleUnloadRssMb` budget — android-phone 1600 MB for both tiers. A regression that stops the unload leaves tail RSS at the warm footprint (≥2.3 GB measured) → **fails loud**. `checkBudgets` gained per-workload check selection; 13 `node --test` cases green.

## Emulator verification (arm64 AVD, Android 15, `CONSTRAINED` — MemTotal ≈ 2.9 GiB at soak time)

Full bundle: `.github/issue-evidence/11760-inference-memory-policy/`. Highlights:

- **Policy fires** — three constrained decisions logged from both the Java probe and the TS bootstrap: `ramClass=CONSTRAINED totalMem=2975MB lmkThreshold=216MB nCtx=4096 idleUnloadMs=120000`.
- **On-device generate works** (after this soak surfaced + fixed the retired-QJL-KV bug): `fast path done, latencyMs=3782`, `cacheTypeK=q8_0 cacheTypeV=f16`.
- **Release reclaims ~700 MB, lossless** — the in-process pressure lever fired twice on-device (`MemAvailable=233MB` and `321MB`, both `ramClass=constrained`). The second correlates exactly in `meminfo-timeline.tsv`: bun agent VmRSS **1.36 GB (loaded) → 640 MB (freed) → 1.5 GB (reloaded on the next request)** while MemAvailable rose ~570 MB — the app was never killed.
- **Acceptance met** — during the soak lmkd fired **38 kills across 28 other packages** under "low watermark is breached", and `ai.elizaos.app` was killed **0 times**; `exit-info` is clean of `reason=3 (LOW_MEMORY)` (its only exits are this session's `USER REQUESTED` force-stops + `PACKAGE UPDATED` reinstalls). This is the #11760 acceptance shape: the app is no longer lmkd's target under real ambient pressure.

## Honest deltas vs the Pixel 6a target

- The session-shared AVD measured `CONSTRAINED` (2.9 GiB — *harder* than the 5.7 GB Pixel 6a) and runs the **CPU path** (no `libggml-vulkan.so` staged → no Mali GL-mtrack term; the identical `resetResident`/`unloadModel` release plumbing is unit-tested on both legs). The idle lever specifically kept losing the race to the pressure lever (correct: the emulator was genuinely pressured) and the app was restarted by sibling sessions every ~2-3 min, so the idle *trigger* on-device is covered by the unit tests rather than an isolated soak capture; the release *mechanism* it shares with pressure is proven on-device.
- The device-exact 10-min Pixel 6a soak (Mali GL mtrack + that device's lmkd tables) is the hardware-lab row → #11734.
- The budget (`maxPostIdleUnloadRssMb=1600`) is a first emulator-informed ceiling; ratchet per BASELINE.md once the Pixel row lands.

41:T17dc,## What

The launcher notifications now work like a modern **iOS Notification Center**: pull down from **anywhere** on the default dashboard to bring the center down, out-of-box default notifications, and no resting pill.

**Pulling brings the real sheet down.** A downward drag anywhere on the dashboard (while the widget list is at the top) **fades in and finger-tracks the actual notification sheet** — the shade descends from the top edge (top-first, header leading). What you drag *is* the sheet. Release past threshold settles it open; a short release retracts it.

- **No resting pill** and **no pull "tag"/affordance** — the sheet itself is the feedback.
- **No header close X** — dismiss via backdrop / grabber / Escape / outside-click.
- **Proper glass**, not flat: a frosted surface (blur + saturate, hairline border, lit top edge, soft drop shadow) on both the mobile sheet and the desktop panel, with notification cards as their own lighter glass tiles.
- **Default notifications out of the box** — *Take the tour*, *Get help*, *Connect your calendar* seed once per agent at boot (`seedOnboardingNotifications`), deep-linking to `/tutorial`, `/help`, `/connectors`.

## How

- **`useNotificationPull`** — a **touch-based** top-overscroll gesture (non-passive `touchmove` + `preventDefault`) so it takes over the scroll under `touch-action: pan-y` (a pointer gesture there is dead on arrival — the compositor claims the vertical drag and fires `pointercancel`). It engages only on a downward, vertical-dominant drag starting at `scrollTop 0`, so real scrolling, widget taps, and the home↔launcher pager are never hijacked.
- **`notification-shell` store** holds the center's open/drag state so the home pull (a different subtree) drives the real sheet live. Tray / deep-link / desktop-button opens go through the same store. The single headless `NotificationCenter` owns the sheet, subscribing to the store and staying mounted through its exit animation.
- The reveal is a top-first `clip-path` + fade + small settle, tracked to the finger while dragging and eased on open/close. The invisible top-edge button (eager-capture `usePullGesture`) stays as the click/keyboard/desktop entry point.

Captures a **real-model** LifeOps benchmark run for the #10721 personal-assistant audit closure, satisfying #11789. The model under test is a **real local model** (eliza-1) — not the deterministic LLM proxy, not `registerCalibratedJudgeFixture`, not a mock standing in for the model.

## How

- Built a `llama.cpp` `llama-server` (submodule `299d5b78b`, gemma4 arch) **CPU-only** — the host NVIDIA GPU is wedged in a runtime-PM error state (`nvidia-smi` device-handle error; needs reboot), so CUDA is unavailable. CPU is real, just slow.
- Served **eliza-1-2b** (gemma-4-E2B) and **eliza-1-4b** (gemma-4-E4B Q8) at an OpenAI-compatible endpoint and drove `packages/benchmarks/lifeops-bench` (LifeOpsBench) with the **Hermes** adapter in **`--mode static`**. Static scoring is deterministic (world `state_hash` + required-output substring) — **no hosted judge / simulated user**.

## Score history (real, recorded)

| Run | Model | Scenarios | pass@1 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| oracle | `perfect` (ground-truth) | 5 | **1.000** |
| smoke | eliza-1-2b (real, CPU) | 5 | **0.000** |
| smoke | eliza-1-4b (real, CPU) | 5 | **0.000** |
| static slice | eliza-1-2b (real, CPU) | 10 | **0.000** |

`perfect`=1.000 on the identical scenarios proves the scorer/world/corpus are valid, so the 0.000 rows are a genuine model/adapter result, not a broken harness.

## Hand-review (why 0.000)

The **eliza-1-4b** model reasons correctly — it picks the right tool with the right arguments every time (e.g. `calendar.get_events(time_range='2026-05-14T09:00:00Z/2026-05-14T10:00:00Z')`, `archive_thread(thread_id='thread_01464')`, iMessage to Hannah with the correct body) — but serializes them in **gemma-native `tool_code`/`print(...)` syntax**, which the Hermes XML `<tool_call>` parser cannot read, so no world mutation occurs. The 2B is weaker (prose / `<think>` blocks, no tool envelope). The recorded score is therefore a **lower bound confounded by an adapter/template mismatch**, not a clean capability measure — documented honestly in the evidence README. The faithful adapter (native `eliza` runtime) drives 40k+ token PA turns and is CPU-infeasible on this GPU-wedged host (attempt captured under `reports/`).

## Evidence

Retained (the #11789 retention-location decision) in `.github/issue-evidence/10721-lifeops-benchmark-history/`: per-run JSON (`scenarios[].turns[]` raw `agent_message`, tokens, latency, `total_score`, `state_hash_match`), stdout logs, backend fingerprint, scenario-runner trajectory bundle, and a README mapping all four #11789 acceptance criteria + skipped-scenario/unavailable-provider reasons.

## Code change

`SCENARIO_TURN_TIMEOUT_MS` env override for the scenario-runner CLI (default stays 120s) so a slow local-model CPU lane can raise the per-turn budget. Verified live — the runtime honored `280000ms` vs the 120000 default.

## AC mapping (#11789)

- [x] Real-model LifeOps/PA benchmark run on current `develop` (b40e60275b), no proxy/mock judge for the model under test.
- [x] Score history + per-scenario artifacts retained where reviewers can inspect them (this evidence dir).
- [x] Run report manually reviewed + summarized in `.github/issue-evidence/10721-lifeops-benchmark-history/`.
- [x] Skipped scenarios / unavailable providers explicitly listed with reasons (GPU/CUDA down → CPU; full 11,220-run corpus → committed subset; live mode → static/no-judge by design; scenario-runner native path → CPU-blocked).
